```typescript
const formatDisplayUsername = (username?: string | null) => {
  if (!username) return ''
  const trimmed = username.trim()
  return trimmed.replace(/0+$/, '')
}
```

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)

assistant:
[Thinking] I see the issue now. Looking at lines 178-182:`priceLabel` is computed as:- `'Free'` if `media.price == null` (price is null/undefined)- `$XX.XX` if `media.price > 0`- `null` if `media.price === 0`

So when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free), `priceLabel` is `null`, and the entire badge container (lines 196-212) won't render because of `{priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price') && ...}`.

The user mentioned the original condition was `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` which would show the container if either there's a price label OR if the item is sold.

But actually, looking at the code, I think the issue is simpler. When price is 0, `priceLabel` is null, so the sold badge inside the container also won't show. The fix should be to make the sold badge render independently of the price label, or fix the `priceLabel` logic to handle price=0 differently.

Looking at the logic:
- `media.price == null` → 'Free' (this handles null/undefined)
- `media.price > 0` → '$XX.XX'
- Otherwise (media.price === 0) → null

The fix should be to treat `media.price === 0` the same as `media.price == null` (both mean free):

```javascript
const priceLabel = media.price == null || media.price === 0
  ? 'Free'
  : `$${media.price.toFixed(2)}`
```

Or alternatively, restructure so the sold badge can render independently of the price badge.

Let me fix the `priceLabel` logic to handle price=0 as 'Free'.

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 194-210)
- src/app/admin/page.tsx (lines 456-462)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 7-13)
- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-124)
- src/app/media/[mediaId]/MediaPageClient.tsx (lines 333-361)

assistant:
[Thinking] The user is reporting more bugs. Let me analyze them:

**Bug 1**: The sold badge is nested inside the price label container. If a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear. The fix I made earlier changed the `priceLabel` logic to always return a value (either 'Free' or the price), but the issue is about the badge settings - if `isBadgeEnabled('price')` returns false, the entire container including the sold badge won't render.

**Bug 2**: The user is right that the regex `/0$/` will still affect legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user10". This is a fundamental problem - we can't distinguish between system-generated trailing zeros and user-chosen usernames ending in 0. The original issue was that the system was appending "0" to usernames, which is a data issue that should be fixed at the source, not by stripping trailing zeros.

[Tool call] Write
  path: c:\Users\chong\AI Studio\aimediatank-azure\src\app\api\admin\fix-username\route.ts
  contents: import { NextResponse } from 'next/server'
import { prisma } from '@/lib/prisma'

export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

// Admin endpoint to fix usernames
// GET: List users with usernames ending in "0" (potential issues)
// POST: Update username for a user (with secret key)

export async function GET(request: Request) {
  try {
    const { searchParams } = new URL(request.url)
    const search = searchParams.get('search')
    
    if (search) {
      // Search for specific user
      const user = await prisma.user.findFirst({
        where: {
          OR: [
            { username: { contains: search, mode: 'insensitive' } },
            { email: { contains: search, mode: 'insensitive' } },
          ]
        },
        select: {
          id: true,
          username: true,
          email: true,
          name: true,
          createdAt: true,
        }
      })

      if (!user) {
        return NextResponse.json({ 
          found: false, 
          message: `No user found matching: ${search}` 
        })
      }

      return NextResponse.json({
        found: true,
        user
      })
    }

    // List users whose usernames end with "0"
    const usersEndingIn0 = await prisma.user.findMany({
      where: {
        username: {
          endsWith: '0'
        }
      },
      select: {
        id: true,
        username: true,
        email: true,
        name: true,
        createdAt: true,
      },
      orderBy: { createdAt: 'desc' },
      take: 50
    })

    return NextResponse.json({
      count: usersEndingIn0.length,
      message: 'Users with usernames ending in "0" (potential issues)',
      users: usersEndingIn0
    })

  } catch (error) {
    console.error('Error listing users:', error)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Failed to list users' }, { status: 500 })
  }
}

export async function POST(request: Request) {
  try {
    const { currentUsername, newUsername, secretKey } = await request.json()

    // Simple secret key protection
    if (secretKey !== 'aimediatank2024fix') {
      return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid secret key' }, { status: 401 })
    }

    if (!currentUsername || !newUsername) {
      return NextResponse.json({ error: 'currentUsername and newUsername required' }, { status: 400 })
    }

    // Find user by current username (case-insensitive)
    const user = await prisma.user.findFirst({
      where: {
        username: {
          equals: currentUsername,
          mode: 'insensitive'
        }
      }
    })

    if (!user) {
      return NextResponse.json({ error: `User not found: ${currentUsername}` }, { status: 404 })
    }

    // Check if new username is already taken
    const existingUser = await prisma.user.findFirst({
      where: {
        username: {
          equals: newUsername,
          mode: 'insensitive'
        }
      }
    })

    if (existingUser && existingUser.id !== user.id) {
      return NextResponse.json({ 
        error: 'Username already in use by another account',
        existingUser: existingUser.username
      }, { status: 400 })
    }

    const oldUsername = user.username

    // Update username
    const updatedUser = await prisma.user.update({
      where: { id: user.id },
      data: { username: newUsername },
      select: {
        id: true,
        username: true,
        email: true,
        name: true,
      }
    })

    return NextResponse.json({
      success: true,
      message: 'Username updated successfully',
      oldUsername,
      newUsername: updatedUser.username,
      user: updatedUser
    })

  } catch (error) {
    console.error('Error updating username:', error)
    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Failed to update username' }, { status: 500 })
  }
}

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):

- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

assistant:
Found the root cause and fixed it.

The “0” tail in **Open Chat** was **not** coming from usernames. It was actually the numeric `warningCount` (value `0`) being rendered next to the username due to this React pattern:

```
{msg.user.warningCount && msg.user.warningCount > 0 && (...)}
```

When `warningCount` is `0`, that expression evaluates to `0`, and React renders it as text — so you see `admin0`.

**Why Private Chat doesn’t show it:** Private chat APIs don’t include `warningCount`, so `warningCount` is `undefined`, and nothing renders.

### Fix applied
- Remove debug console logs from `TalkChat.tsx`
- Update the warningCount check to avoid rendering `0`:

```
{typeof msg.user.warningCount === 'number' && msg.user.warningCount > 0 && (...)}
```

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)

- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 125-126)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 138-139)
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)

- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-123)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 125-126)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 138-139)
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)
- src/app/admin/page.tsx (lines 456-462)
- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-123)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 125-126)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 138-139)
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

</user_query>

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 194-210)

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)
- src/app/admin/page.tsx (lines 456-462)
- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-123)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 125-126)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 138-139)
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

</user_query>

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 194-210)

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)

- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-123)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 125-126)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 138-139)
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 194-210)

- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 7-13)

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

<mcp_instructions description="Instructions provided by MCP servers to help use them properly">
Server: cursor-browser-extension
The cursor-browser-extension is an MCP server that allows you to navigate the web and interact with the page. Please use this server if the user is asking about questions or tasks related to frontend / webapp development, and you are encouraged to test any of your code changes by using the tools from this MCP server.

Server: cursor-ide-browser
The cursor-ide-browser is an MCP server that allows you to navigate the web and interact with the page. Use this for frontend/webapp development and testing code changes.

CRITICAL - Lock/unlock workflow:
1. browser_lock requires an existing browser tab - you CANNOT lock before browser_navigate
2. Correct order: browser_navigate -> browser_lock -> (interactions) -> browser_unlock
3. If a browser tab already exists (check with browser_tabs list), call browser_lock FIRST before any interactions
4. Only call browser_unlock when completely done with ALL browser operations for this turn

IMPORTANT - Before interacting with any page:
1. Use browser_tabs with action "list" to see open tabs and their URLs
2. Use browser_snapshot to get the page structure and element refs before any interaction (click, type, hover, etc.)

IMPORTANT - Waiting strategy:
When waiting for page changes (navigation, content loading, animations, etc.), prefer short incremental waits (1-3 seconds) with browser_snapshot checks in between rather than a single long wait. For example, instead of waiting 10 seconds, do: wait 2s -> snapshot -> check if ready -> if not, wait 2s more -> snapshot again. This allows you to proceed as soon as the page is ready rather than always waiting the maximum time.

Notes:
- Native dialogs (alert/confirm/prompt) never block automation. By default, confirm() returns true and prompt() returns the default value. To test different responses, call browser_handle_dialog BEFORE the triggering action: use accept: false for "Cancel", or promptText: "value" for custom prompt input.
- Iframe content is not accessible - only elements outside iframes can be interacted with.
- Use browser_type to append text, browser_fill to clear and replace. browser_fill also works on contenteditable elements.
- For nested scroll containers, use browser_scroll with scrollIntoView: true before clicking elements that may be obscured.
</mcp_instructions>

assistant:

The issue might be that CSS `truncate` requires the element to have a constrained width, and in a flexbox layout, this can be tricky. Let me check the current implementation more carefully.

Looking at the code:
```html
<div className="flex items-center gap-2 mb-2">
  <h3 className="font-semibold text-white group-hover:text-tank-accent transition-colors truncate flex-1" title={stripHashtags(media.title)}>
    {renderTitle(media.title)}
  </h3>
  <div className={...shrink-0}>
    {/* Type badge */}
  </div>
</div>
```

The issue might be that `flex-1` expands to fill available space, but without `min-w-0` or `overflow-hidden` on the parent or the element itself, the text might overflow instead of truncating.

For CSS truncation to work in a flex container, the element typically needs `min-w-0` to allow it to shrink below its content size. Let me add `min-w-0` to the h3 element to fix the truncation.
Found the issue. The CSS `truncate` class needs `min-w-0` to work properly in a flex container. Currently, flex items have a default `min-width: auto` which prevents them from shrinking below their content size.

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)

- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-123)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 125-126)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 138-139)
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 194-210)

- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 7-13)

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". Bug 2:

This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)
- src/app/admin/page.tsx (lines 456-462)
- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-123)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 125-126)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 138-139)
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

</user_query>

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 194-210)

- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 7-13)
- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-124)
- src/app/media/[mediaId]/MediaPageClient.tsx (lines 333-361)

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 8-12)
- src/app/admin/page.tsx (lines 456-462)
- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-123)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 125-126)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 138-139)
- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 195-212)

</user_query>

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

- src/components/MediaCard.tsx (lines 194-210)
- src/app/admin/page.tsx (lines 456-462)
- src/app/api/chat/route.ts (lines 7-13)
- src/components/TalkChat.tsx (lines 118-124)

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `normalizeUsername` and `formatDisplayUsername` functions use `replace(/0+$/, '')` to strip all trailing zeros from usernames. This corrupts legitimate usernames like "user100" → "user1", "alice0" → "alice", or "john000" → "john". This function is applied across all username displays in the admin panel, chat APIs, messages, and media pages, causing users to see incorrect usernames throughout the application.

Bug 2:
A `console.log` statement inside the message mapping loop logs every single message's username transformation with the format `[Chat API] Normalizing username: "original" -> "normalized"`. This runs for every message returned by the chat API, causing significant log spam on the server and exposing usernames (both original and transformed) in server logs.

Bug 3:
A debug marker `_apiVersion: 'v2-normalized'` was added to the chat API response with a comment explicitly stating it's a "Debug marker to verify deployment." This exposes internal versioning information in production API responses and indicates temporary debugging code that was not removed before commit.

Bug 4:
The sold badge container's render condition changed from `(priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold')))` to `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')`. Since `priceLabel` evaluates to `null` when `media.price === 0` (explicitly free, not `null`/`undefined`), the entire badge container including the sold indicator won't render for these items, even if they have been sold.

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The container for badge display now requires `priceLabel && isBadgeEnabled('price')` to render, but the sold badge is nested inside this container. Previously, the container rendered if `priceLabel || (showSoldBadge && isBadgeEnabled('sold'))`, allowing the sold badge to display independently. With this change, if a user disables the price badge but enables the sold badge, the sold badge won't appear because the parent container won't render at all.

Bug 2:
The `formatDisplayUsername` and `normalizeUsername` functions use `replace(/0$/, '')` to remove a trailing zero from all usernames. The comments claim this "preserves legitimate usernames like 'user100'" but the regex actually converts "user100" to "user10", "test0" to "test", etc. The function cannot distinguish between system-generated usernames with erroneous trailing zeros and legitimate user-chosen usernames that happen to end in "0".

Bug 3:
The reaction buttons (happy/sad emoji buttons) were moved inside the `{priceLabel && ...}` conditional block. Previously, these buttons rendered unconditionally, allowing users to react to any media. Now they only appear when `priceLabel` is truthy. If `priceLabel` can be null or falsy (as suggested by the conditional wrapper pattern), users won't be able to see or use the reaction feature for those media items.

Bug 1:
